Le panneau montre, pour chaque groupe, ce qu'il faut pour trancher : nombre de lots, poids actif, nombre de séances, numéros d'inventaire et date de dernière modification. Quand deux fiches partagent un numéro d'inventaire qu'on ne retrouve nulle part ailleurs dans la cave, il vous le signale (« probablement la même boîte »). Vous désignez celle à garder : ses lots et ses séances récupèrent ceux de l'autre, qui part à la corbeille — récupérable 30 jours.

Les numéros d'inventaire ne sont jamais réécrits — ils sont inscrits sur la boîte physique. Deux lots déplacés peuvent donc porter le même numéro : c'est précisément le signe qu'il s'agit de la même boîte.

15. Mises à jour

Paramètres → onglet Application → bouton Vérifier les mises à jour (sous le nom et la version de l'app). Si une nouvelle version est disponible, un encart Mettre à jour apparaît : confirmez, et l'application se retélécharge.

La mise à jour ne touche que le cache de l'application : votre cave, vos photos et vos notes ne sont pas concernées.

En cas d'erreur lors de la mise à jour

Si l'application reste bloquée sur l'écran de chargement après une mise à jour, attendez qu'un correctif soit disponible, puis cliquez sur le lien Rien ne se passe ? Réparer l'application → qui apparaît automatiquement après quelques secondes. Cela réinitialise le cache et recharge l'application depuis le réseau.

Cette réparation ne supprime pas vos données. Si le serveur est injoignable, elle refuse de s'exécuter et n'efface rien : reconnectez-vous, puis réessayez.

16. Dépannage

Bandeaux d'alerte de l'application

L'application affiche plusieurs bandeaux selon le contexte :

  • Bandeau rouge (haut d'écran) — erreur de sauvegarde. Cliquez dessus pour ouvrir les Paramètres et faire un export JSON de secours. Causes possibles : quota épuisé, stockage bloqué par le navigateur, navigation privée.
  • Bandeau ambré (bas d'écran) — session Drive expirée (voir section 14). Deux actions : Reconnecter, ×.
  • Bandeau vert (haut d'écran) — rappel de sauvegarde : plus de 30 jours sans aucun export ni sauvegarde cloud. Toute sa surface ouvre les Paramètres → Données.
  • Bandeau ambré « stockage presque plein » — l'appareil approche de son quota. Libérez de l'espace ou supprimez des photos ; masquable 7 jours.
  • Bandeau rouge « photo » — une image n'a pas pu être ajoutée (au-delà de 20 Mo, ou fichier illisible).
  • Bandeau doré (haut d'écran) — une dégustation est en cours et vous êtes sur une autre vue. Tapez ▶ Reprendre pour revenir à la vue plein écran.
  • Bandeau rouge spécial dégustation — séance active depuis plus de 90 minutes (voir section 11, Garde-fou 90 minutes).
  • Toast d'annulation (8s) — affiché en bas après chaque suppression (tabac, pipe, accessoire, envie, séance, lot). Bouton Annuler pour annuler immédiatement le geste pendant les 8 secondes. Filet de sécurité instantané, en complément de la corbeille 30 jours (voir section 17).

Erreur de sauvegarde — détails

Causes possibles :

  • Quota épuisé — supprimez des photos ou libérez de l'espace sur l'appareil.
  • iOS Safari — stockage bloqué : Réglages → Safari → Avancé → Données de sites web.
  • Android Chrome — stockage bloqué : Chrome → Paramètres → Confidentialité et sécurité → Paramètres des sites → Stockage.
  • Navigation privée — les données sont perdues à la fermeture de l'onglet ; l'application n'est pas utilisable en navigation privée.

Prérequis du navigateur

Pour fonctionner correctement, l'application nécessite :

  • JavaScript activé (actif par défaut dans tous les navigateurs)
  • Stockage local autorisé — toutes vos données sont conservées via localStorage. Sur iOS Safari : Réglages → Safari → Avancé → Bloquer tous les cookies doit être désactivé. Sur Android Chrome : vérifiez que les cookies/le stockage ne sont pas bloqués pour ce site (Chrome → Paramètres → Paramètres des sites). En navigation privée, les données sont effacées à la fermeture de l'onglet.
  • Installation en mode PWA — sur iOS, utilisez uniquement Safari (pas Chrome ni Firefox) : icône Partager → Sur l'écran d'accueil. Sur Android, utilisez Chrome : menu ⋮ → Ajouter à l'écran d'accueil.

L'application reste bloquée sur « Chargement… »

Si l'écran de chargement ne disparaît pas après quelques secondes, un lien Rien ne se passe ? Réparer l'application → apparaît automatiquement. Cliquez dessus : cela efface le cache du service worker et recharge l'application depuis le réseau.

Cette réparation ne supprime pas vos données — seul le cache de l'application est réinitialisé. Si le serveur est injoignable, elle refuse de s'exécuter et n'efface rien : reconnectez-vous, puis réessayez.

17. Corbeille (30 jours)

Toutes les suppressions de l'app sont réversibles. Quand vous tapez sur l'icône poubelle d'un tabac, d'une pipe, d'un accessoire, d'une envie, d'une séance ou d'un lot, l'élément n'est pas effacé : il part en corbeille, où il reste 30 jours avant suppression définitive automatique.

Comment l'ouvrir

Dès qu'au moins un élément se trouve en corbeille, une icône poubelle ambre apparaît dans la barre du haut de toutes les pages principales (Cave, Tabacs, Pipes, Access., Journal, Stats) ainsi que sur la fiche d'un tabac, d'une pipe ou d'un accessoire. Tapez dessus pour ouvrir le modal Corbeille. Si la corbeille est vide, l'icône disparaît — pas de bouton mort.

Restaurer

  • Restaurer un élément — icône restaurer (flèche circulaire) sur sa ligne. Il retrouve sa place dans l'inventaire avec toutes ses données (lots, photos, notes…).
  • Tout restaurer — bouton en bas du modal. Restaure tous les éléments en une fois.
  • Sélection multiple — bouton Sélection en haut du modal pour passer en mode case-à-cocher. Cochez les éléments à restaurer, puis tapez Restaurer la sélection (N).

Supprimer définitivement

  • × sur une ligne — supprime cet élément sans attendre les 30 jours. Pas de confirmation : si vous hésitez, utilisez plutôt Restaurer.
  • Vider la corbeille — bouton en bas. Confirme avant de tout effacer (la seule confirmation conservée car l'action contourne le filet des 30 jours).

Cas particuliers

  • Séances et tabac/pipe supprimés : les séances historiques sont conservées même quand leur tabac ou pipe est supprimé. Le journal affiche alors la photo et le nom tels qu'ils étaient au moment de la suppression (instantané figé). Recréer un tabac ou une pipe avec le même nom ne ré-attache pas automatiquement les anciennes séances (les identifiants internes restent uniques).
  • Lots supprimés : les séances qui pointent vers eux conservent leur lien tant que la corbeille n'est pas vidée. Restaurer le lot ré-attache les séances proprement.
  • Annuler immédiatement : juste après chaque suppression, un toast en bas affiche un bouton Annuler pendant 8 secondes (filet de sécurité instantané, à côté du filet long terme qu'est la corbeille).
Les sauvegardes JSON et Drive incluent les éléments en corbeille — vous retrouvez donc ce filet de sécurité de 30 jours même après une réinstallation ou une restauration. L'export CSV, lui, est un instantané de l'inventaire vivant : il ne contient pas la corbeille.

18. Tous les paramètres, et ce qu'ils changent

Un récapitulatif de bout en bout, pour répondre à « où se règle ceci ? » sans relire le guide. Chaque réglage est déjà décrit dans sa section, où vous trouverez le raisonnement ; ici il n'y a que l'endroit, l'effet et la valeur par défaut. On y accède par l'icône engrenage, en haut de l'accueil.

Onglet Préférences

C'est l'onglet qui s'ouvre en premier. Rien ici ne touche à vos données : ce sont des réglages d'affichage et de comportement, propres à cet appareil — mais ils voyagent dans vos sauvegardes et une restauration en mode Remplacer installera ceux du fichier.

  • Langue — Toute l'interface. Chaque langue se télécharge à son premier choix puis reste disponible hors ligne. Défaut : celle du navigateur, sinon anglais.
  • Taille du texte — Redimensionne l'interface entière d'un coup, pas seulement les paragraphes. Défaut : Moyen.
  • Thème — La couleur d'accent et l'ambiance : Laiton, Bleu acier ou Anglais. Défaut : Laiton.
  • Mode — Sombre ou Clair (fond parchemin). Indépendant du thème. Défaut : Sombre.
  • Format de date — L'affichage des dates et celles de l'export CSV. Défaut : FR (15.03.2024).
  • Unité de poids — Le suffixe partout : lots, poids des pipes, journal. Libellé seul — une valeur déjà saisie n'est pas reconvertie. Défaut : g.
  • Unité de longueur — La longueur des pipes. Libellé seul, comme ci-dessus. Défaut : mm.
  • Devise — Le symbole de tous les prix. Libellé seul : aucun taux de change. Défaut : €.
  • Grammage par défaut — Le poids proposé à l'ajout d'une séance, quand la pipe choisie n'a pas de dimensions de foyer. Sinon l'app estime depuis le foyer et la coupe. Défaut : 3.
  • Listes groupées — L'affichage initial des listes, groupé par marque ou à plat. Vous pouvez toujours basculer ponctuellement. Défaut : Groupé.
  • Comptabilité — Si l'app suit les quantités. Désactivée, le champ Grammage disparaît des séances, plus aucune déduction ni passage auto en Terminé — mais l'ouverture d'un lot en cave reste, c'est un événement physique. Défaut : Activée.
  • Rappels d'entretien — La pastille « à entretenir » sur les pipes et la section de l'accueil. Le carnet reste utilisable même désactivés. Défaut : Activés.
  • Seuil d'entretien — Le nombre de séances sans nettoyage avant le rappel. Seuls les nettoyages léger et complet remettent le compteur à zéro. Défaut : 5 séances.
  • Seuil stock bas — Sous ce poids restant, un tabac passe dans « À surveiller » et dans la liste de courses — sauf s'il est marqué « à ne pas reprendre ». Dans votre unité de poids. Défaut : 25 g.
  • Sections — Masque Accessoires, Journal ou Statistiques : la section quitte la barre du bas et le tableau de bord. Les données restent. Défaut : toutes visibles.

Onglet Données

Tout ce qui fait entrer ou sortir des données. C'est le seul onglet dont les actions sont irréversibles.

  • Destination — Dropbox ou Google Drive. La garde multi-appareils ne compare qu'à l'intérieur d'une même destination : choisissez la même sur tous vos appareils. Défaut : Dropbox.
  • Sauvegarde auto cloud — Une sauvegarde silencieuse part quelques secondes après chaque modification, et en quittant l'app. Chaque appareil garde son propre fichier. Défaut : Désactivée.
  • Chiffrer les sauvegardes cloud — Chiffre chaque sauvegarde cloud avec une phrase secrète, avant l'envoi. La phrase n'est jamais stockée et ne se récupère pas. Défaut : Désactivé.
  • Nom de cet appareil — Rend cet appareil identifiable dans le panneau des sauvegardes et dans le nom des fichiers cloud. Pas dans leur contenu. Défaut : vide.
  • Catalogue de référence — Charge, exporte ou retire votre fichier de référence, et le sauvegarde dans le cloud à part. N'ajoute jamais rien à votre cave — voir section 4. Défaut : aucun.
  • Assistant IA — Fournisseur, modèle, clé API, et la source prioritaire : votre catalogue d'abord, ou l'IA d'abord. « Auto » laisse l'app prendre le modèle le moins cher et en changer s'il est retiré. Défaut : Catalogue d'abord ; aucune clé.
  • Exclure les clés API — Empêche votre clé de partir dans les exports et les sauvegardes. À laisser activé si vous partagez un fichier. Défaut : Activé.
  • Doublons — N'apparaît que s'il y a des fiches à fusionner — le nombre est la raison d'aller voir. Défaut : caché.
  • Effacer toutes les données — Vide la cave, les photos, les clés API et la connexion cloud de cet appareil. Vos sauvegardes déjà dans le cloud ne sont pas touchées. Irréversible. Défaut : —.

Onglet Application

  • Installer cette app — La marche à suivre pour l'ajouter à l'écran d'accueil, propre à votre navigateur. Défaut : —.
  • Vérifier les mises à jour — Interroge le serveur, puis recharge l'app en vidant son cache. Vos données ne sont pas touchées. Défaut : —.
  • Diagnostic — Surveille la cohérence interne. Vert tant que tout va bien ; en cas d'anomalie, il liste les dernières occurrences et propose de les copier. Défaut : —.

Onglet Aide

Des liens, aucun réglage : ce mode d'emploi, les nouveautés de la version, la politique de confidentialité, les licences des composants tiers, et de quoi nous écrire.

Ce qui n'est pas dans les Paramètres. Certains réglages vivent là où ils s'appliquent, parce qu'ils ne concernent qu'un écran : le tri et les filtres de chaque liste, le pliage des groupes, l'ordre de la wishlist, et le repli des sections de ce guide. Ils sont retenus d'une visite à l'autre, mais ils ne voyagent pas dans les sauvegardes.

1. Installing on the home screen

Install the app like a real app — it then works offline.

iPhone / iPad (Safari)

  1. Open t-cellar.app in Safari
  2. Tap Share → Add to Home Screen
  3. Confirm

Android (Chrome)

  1. Open t-cellar.app in Chrome
  2. Either tap the Install button that appears in Settings → App as soon as Chrome considers the app installable, or use the ⋮ menu → Add to Home Screen

2. Core concepts

The app is organized around two main objects:

  • Tobacco — a reference (e.g. Peterson Nightcap). A tobacco can have zero or more lots.
  • Lot — a specific tin or quantity purchased on a given date. The lot carries the status and aging history.

This distinction lets you track multiple tins of the same tobacco at different stages (one cellaring, one open in a jar) while sharing a single set of notes and description.

A third word turns up everywhere: the session — one pipe smoked, that is to say a tobacco, a pipe and a duration. Most smokers would simply call it a bowl, the contents of one chamber. It is not an object you own, it is what you do with what you own: the unit the app counts, weighs and deducts from your lots.

The tobacco and the lot form your inventory: what you actually own. Alongside it, the app can also use a third thing, of a different nature:

  • The reference catalogue — an optional file that you load and that describes commercial blends (family, cut, composition, description), while saying nothing about your tins. It holds no weight, no price and no date. It is there to fill in your entries faster, never to replace them.
Inventory and catalogue do not mix. Loading a catalogue adds nothing to your cellar; adding a tobacco of your own does not change the catalogue. The two are nevertheless loaded from the same Settings → Data screen, which is the most common confusion — section 4 is devoted to them.

3. Tobacco lifecycle

●PurchaseNew lot
→
●CellarSealed tin
→
●JarTin opened
→
●FinishedLot empty

Cellar

A lot you've just bought enters Cellar directly: a sealed tin aging in storage. Age is calculated from the production date (if you set it), otherwise from the purchase date. A lot's fields: weight, purchase and production dates, box number, storage location (free text, e.g. "Cabinet A · shelf 2"), price and seller. The weight unit (g or oz) is set in Settings → Preferences.

Jar

When you open a tin: tap the lot row → Edit → status Jar. The opening date fills in by itself, and the weight updates as you log sessions.

Finished

Empty lot → Finished (the end date is recorded automatically). It leaves the active stock but stays in history (Show finished). A lot that closes on its own counts as consumed: nothing asks you for a reason at the time. If you actually threw the tin away or gave it away, open its row → Edit and pick:

  • ✓ Consumed — smoked.
  • 🚮 Disposed — thrown or given away unsmoked. The lot leaves the Finished lot counter for the inventory's Disposed chip, and it will never reactivate by itself again.
"Disposed" is a marker, not an accounting exclusion. The lot's price stays in Spending, and the sessions already logged against it stay in the smoked tobacco — they really did happen.

Reactivate

A finished lot can come back to life via Reactivate (a mistake, or a rediscovered tin). It returns to the status by which it entered the inventory: Cellar for a sealed tin, Jar for a lot created directly as a jar. A lot marked 🚮 Disposed is reactivated this way, by hand, and this way only.

You can also create a lot directly as "Jar" or "Finished" to record past history.

Accounting (quantity tracking)

A global Accounting toggle, in Settings → Preferences → Accounting, decides whether the app tracks the amounts of tobacco smoked. On by default.

  • On — each session (and each tasting) deducts its weight from the selected Jar lot. When a jar reaches 0 g it switches by itself to Finished — ✓ Consumed. If you log a session against a Cellar (sealed) lot, a confirmation offers to open the lot (Cellar → Jar, dated today) before deducting. The Weight field is required in the session form, and these weights feed the "smoked tobacco" charts in Statistics.
  • Off — the Weight field disappears from the session form and from the live tasting: no deduction, no automatic Finished switch, and these sessions don't enter the smoked-weight charts (Statistics show a heads-up while the option is off). Choose this if you just want to log what and when you smoke, without tracking amounts.
Note: even with Accounting off, opening a Cellar lot into a Jar (Cellar → Jar) still happens — it's a real physical event (you opened a tin), governed by the lot's status, not by quantity tracking.
The default session weight (which pre-fills the Weight field of a new session) is also set in Settings → Preferences. In addition, when you pick a pipe whose chamber dimensions (diameter + depth) are filled in, the app estimates the weight automatically from the chamber volume and the selected tobacco's cut (a dense flake weighs more than an airy ribbon at the same volume). The pipe shape is factored in too: a conical chamber (Acorn, Cutty, Dublin, Horn, Pear, Pickaxe, Tulip, Woodstock, Zulu — and, more mildly, Bulldog, Bullmoose, Rhodesian) holds a bit less than a straight one of the same dimensions. Without chamber dimensions, the default weight above applies. The estimate stays editable by hand.

Where to start

You now have the two objects and their cycle. In order, on your first day:

  1. Set your units and your currency — Settings → Preferences. This is not a matter of comfort: see the warning below.
  2. Add a first tobacco — the + at the top of the Tobaccos tab. Three ways to do it in section 5; the most manual one depends on nothing.
  3. Fill in its lot — weight, price, dates, from its page (section 6). The lot is what carries everything.
  4. Set up a backup — section 14. Your data lives in the browser; a backup is what protects it.
Units and currency are labels, not conversions. A value already entered is never converted: if you recorded 100 in grams and switch to ounces, the app will display "100 oz". Choose them before you start entering data — afterwards, you will have to correct everything by hand.

The next section describes the reference catalogue. It is optional and the app ships none: if you do not have one yet, you can skip it and come back later.

4. The reference catalogue

The app handles two different things, both of them loaded from Settings → Data, and they must not be confused:

  • Your inventory — your own tins. Lots, weights, prices, purchase dates, sessions smoked. This is your cellar: you edit it constantly and it travels in your backups.
  • The reference catalogue — a file of descriptive entries (brand, name, family, cut, composition, description) that describes none of your tins in particular. It holds no weight, no price and no date: it says what a blend is, not what you own of it.

The catalogue is optional and the app works entirely without it: you can type your whole cellar in by hand. It brings one thing only, but that one thing saves time — never having to retype the characteristics of a blend you already know.

No catalogue ships with the app. The catalogue is your file: you compose it, you correct it, and it holds only what you have put in it. As long as you have loaded none, the screens that use it tell you so and point you back here — nothing is broken.

What the catalogue changes once loaded

  • Auto-fill in the tobacco and wish forms consults it (see section 12).
  • A Catalogue page becomes browsable, with search and filters.
  • You can compare two or three blends side by side.
  • You can apply the catalogue's data in one pass to the entries you already own.
The catalogue never changes your cellar on its own. Loading it adds no tobacco to your inventory, removes none, and touches none of your lots. All it does is open up the possibilities above, which you then trigger yourself. Conversely, entering or deleting a tobacco of your own does not change the catalogue: it is read-only.

Getting and loading your catalogue

  1. Go to Settings → Data → Reference catalogue.
  2. Tap Download the template: you get an empty CSV file, with the header row and one example row.
  3. Fill it in using a spreadsheet — one row per blend.
  4. Come back to the app and tap Load a catalogue (.csv), then pick your file.

Loading a new file replaces the previous one — the catalogue is not cumulative, unlike the import of your own tobaccos. The Remove the catalogue button deletes it; your cellar is untouched, and you can always load it again from your file.

The file format

The CSV template carries the twenty columns the app can read, with one example row. Keep the header row: columns are matched by name, not by position, and a column you do not use can stay empty.

  • brand_key — Required. The brand. Together with blend_name it is the row's identity: two rows sharing the pair are duplicates and only the first is kept. A row missing either one is skipped.
  • blend_name — Required. The blend's name.
  • brand_name — The brand as displayed, when it differs from the key.
  • category — Fixed taxonomy. One of the app's families (list in section 5).
  • cut — Fixed taxonomy. One of the app's cuts (list in section 5).
  • force, roomNote, taste — An integer from 0 to 5. Empty = unknown.
  • agingMax — Cellaring years: a number (10) or a range (10-15). Read only for a category the app has no default duration for — that is, Other, or a category it does not recognise. Everywhere else the family's duration wins and the column's value is not kept: that is deliberate, so as not to freeze a constant into your cellar, but it does mean there is no point filling this column in for a Virginia or a Latakia.
  • blend — The composition, free text ("Virginia, Perique").
  • description_fr … description_pt — One column per language. The app shows yours, else English, else French, else the first one filled: a single one is enough.
  • brand_aliases, blend_aliases — Other names, semicolon-separated. This is what makes a differently-labelled tin resolve.
  • lastReviewed, reviewValidated — Your review notes. Kept as-is, never read by the app.
Two columns must follow the app's taxonomy: category and cut. An unrecognised value is not rejected — it is kept verbatim and the load report names it — but the blend is then only half understood: the family files it nowhere (no maturity band, no default cellaring age), the cut weighs nothing (the bowl-weight estimate falls back to the generic value), the value is not translated and does not exist in the form's dropdown — so opening and saving that tobacco rewrites your value. Write the value exactly as the entry form shows it — both lists are in section 5. Common trade labels are accepted on top of them (Navy Cut, Cigar Leaf, Krumble Kake…).
After every load the screen tells you what it read: how many blends and brands, rows skipped for want of a brand or a name, duplicates dropped, and any family or cut value it did not recognise. Read those lines — a catalogue that silently lost a third of its rows looks exactly like one that loaded fine. The Check my catalogue button just below runs the same check and this time gives the line number of each problem, so you can fix it in your spreadsheet.

That check judges only what the app is able to judge: the two required columns and the two fixed-taxonomy columns. It does not read your descriptions and does not verify that your data is accurate — it is your file.

Browsing the catalogue

From the Tobaccos tab, tap the 📖 icon at the top right: the Catalogue page opens. It is read-only and shows only the contents of your file, never your cellar.

  • Tolerant search: catalogue numbers ("Solani 131" → "Solani Red Label"), partial names ("Peterson Sher" → "Sherlock Holmes"), brands typed differently ("MacBaren", "G L Pease", "Cornell and Diehl") and small typos ("Capstain" → Capstan). The same tolerance powers duplicate detection in your inventory.
  • Filters by family or by brand, and a brand-grouped display.
  • A badge ✓ owned (green) or ★ wished (amber) on each row shows what you already have — it is the one place where the two worlds meet, and purely to inform you.
  • When a blend answers to several names, its card lists them under the title, after Also known as — useful when your tin is labelled differently from the catalogue. Only names that are not already in the title are shown.

From a catalogue card, two buttons move the blend from the reference into your cellar: Add to my inventory and Add to my wishlist (your wishes, section 9). That is the fastest way to enter a tobacco — see section 5.

Comparing blends

The “Compare blends” row, at the top of the Catalogue page just above the search field — or the chart icon in the top bar, or the button at the bottom of a blend's card — puts two or three blends side by side. You can mix the sources freely: a tobacco from your cellar, a catalogue blend you do not own, a wish from your list.

The columns do not all answer the same questions, and that is deliberate. The factual half — family, cut, composition, strength, room note, taste, ageing — exists for every source, and that is where a purchase is decided. The half about your own experience — your rating, stock, oldest lot, sessions, average session rating, cost per bowl, aromas you perceived — exists only for what you own: on a catalogue blend those rows stay at "—". The rows that differ are highlighted.

Applying the catalogue data to your entries

Two routes, depending on whether you want to handle one entry or your whole cellar. In both cases it is your entries that are modified; the catalogue itself never moves.

A single entry. Open it in edit mode (Edit button on a tobacco or a wish). If the brand + name match a catalogue entry and at least one field differs, a "Catalog update available" banner appears at the top of the form and lists the gaps, your value and the catalogue's side by side:

  • Sync replaces your values with the catalogue's in one tap (you can still tweak them before saving).
  • Keep my values hides the banner for that entry.

The whole cellar at once. The button at the top right of the Catalogue page compares your inventory and your wishlist against the catalogue, then updates the factual fields in a single pass: category, cut, composition, strength, room note, taste, maximum ageing, description. The app first shows you what would change (how many entries, how many fields); nothing is written until you confirm.

Your personal data is never touched: tasting notes, rating, "don't rebuy", photo, lots (weights, prices, dates), collections, the wishlist's personal note. Name and brand are left alone too — that is how you recognise your tin, and it is also the key used to avoid duplicates on import. Entries absent from the catalogue stay unchanged, and an "Undo" toast puts everything back for a few seconds after the pass.
Pin an entry you curated by hand. At the very bottom of a tobacco or wishlist form, the “Never overwrite from the catalogue” checkbox puts that entry out of the bulk update's reach: it is skipped entirely, and the catalogue is not even consulted for it. Useful when you corrected a composition yourself, measured a strength, or rewrote a description. The confirmation dialog tells you how many entries are pinned. It does not block the entry-by-entry sync above: there you see the difference on screen before accepting.

Backing up the catalogue

The catalogue is not included in your cellar backups — not in the JSON export, not in the ZIP, not in the automatic backup. That is deliberate: it can weigh several megabytes and only changes once in a while, whereas your cellar is saved on every edit.

It therefore has two buttons of its own, in the same settings section: Back up the catalogue to the cloud and Fetch the catalogue from the cloud. It travels in its own file and is only sent when you ask for it. On a new device, fetching is available even when no catalogue is loaded — which is precisely where it is useful.

Keep the original CSV on your side too: it is the file you edit in your spreadsheet, and the Export my catalogue button hands it back to you exactly as the app holds it.

5. Adding a tobacco

Adding a tobacco means creating an entry in your inventory: a reference you own, to which you will then attach one or more lots. Three ways to do it, from the fastest to the most manual. They produce exactly the same entry and everything stays editable afterwards — pick whichever suits what you have at hand.

  • ① you have loaded a reference catalogue and the blend is in it;
  • ② you want the app to look up the characteristics for you, in your catalogue or online;
  • ③ you type everything yourself — the route that depends on nothing.

① From the catalogue (fastest)

This route assumes you have loaded a reference catalogue — see section 4. If you have none, go to ② or ③: you are missing nothing, entry is simply longer.

  1. From the Tobaccos tab, tap the 📖 icon at the top right: the Catalogue page opens.
  2. Find your blend: search bar ("Capstan blue", "Solani 131", "FVF"… the search is tolerant), category or brand filter, or the brand-grouped list.
  3. Tap the row for a preview, then "Add to my inventory": a pre-filled mini-form opens, everything editable.
  4. Save → the tobacco is created with a first lot in Cellar, and you return to the catalogue to keep going.
The catalogue is not modified by the operation: you have copied its values into a fresh entry, which is yours. Correcting that entry afterwards changes nothing in the reference file.
The "★ Add to my wishlist" button does the same but files the entry under your wishlist. A badge ✓ owned (green) or ★ wished (amber) on each row shows what you already have.
The price, purchase date and lot details are added afterwards from the tobacco's page — see Managing lots.
Already typed a tobacco by hand? You can pull the catalogue's values onto an existing entry: open it in edit mode and a "Catalog update available" banner will offer to sync the divergent fields — see section 4.

② Entry with auto-fill

Tap the + at the top of the Tobaccos tab, enter at least the brand and name, then the Search button on the Auto-fill card. The app fills category, cut, blend, strength, room note, taste, max age and description (all editable). By default it consults your catalogue first when one is loaded (section 4), then your AI assistant if the blend is not in it; the Preferred source setting lets you reverse that order, see AI Assistant. With no catalogue and no API key, that button has nothing to query and tells you so — manual entry ③ is always available. You can also scan the tin to pre-fill brand and name automatically.

Often you will not even have to tap Search. As soon as the brand and name you are typing match a blend in your catalogue, a green banner appears right under the Brand field: "This blend is in your catalogue", with a Fill the sheet button. One tap and everything is filled in, with no network and no API key. It sits there, rather than at the top of the form, because once the keyboard is up the top of the screen has gone. The banner folds away if you ignore it.

③ Fully manual entry

FieldDescription
Name *Tobacco name — required
Brand *Manufacturer — required as well: as long as either one is missing, the Save button stays greyed out
TypeAmerican, English, English aromatic, Aromatic, Balkan, Burley, Cavendish, Cigar, Dark Fired, Scottish, Lakeland, Latakia, Oriental, Perique, Turkish, VaPer, Virginia, Virginia/Burley, Virginia/Latakia, Other
CutBroken Flake, Coarse Cut, Coins, Crumble Cake, Cube Cut, Curly Cut, Flake, Loose Cut, Plug, Pressed, Ready Rubbed, Ribbon, Rope, Rough Cut, Shag, Sliced, Twist, Other
BlendLeaf varieties description
DescriptionGeneral notes
Tobacco notesYour personal impressions of this tobacco
ImageImport a photo button (from your library)
Strength ★Nicotine strength — 1 star (mild) to 5 (very full)
Room Note ★Aroma produced while smoking — 1 (unnoticeable) to 5 (very strong)
Taste ★Flavour intensity — 1 (light) to 5 (very pronounced)
Abbreviations (cards)On the list cards, Strength / Room Note / Taste are shown as their initials, per the app language: S · R · T
Rating (★)0 to 5 stars — your personal appreciation
Rebuy?Yes / No / ? — reminder for future purchases
Max cellar age (yrs)Maximum recommended cellaring duration in years (optional). Can be suggested automatically by the AI.

On save, a first Cellar lot is created with today's date.

Aging (cellar lots only). If you set a max cellar age and a cellar lot exceeds it, a red ⚠ Overaged badge appears (on the card, the lot row and the detail). An amber ⚠ Near peak badge precedes it when the lot approaches its ideal window. These alerts apply only to cellar (sealed) lots: a jar lot is an opened tobacco — it no longer "ages" the same way and instead shows a neutral "Open · duration" marker (how long it's been open), information rather than an alert (you're free to age in a jar). Home sums up the cellar lots in the "Cellar maturity" bar: one band per state, and tapping a band opens the inventory filtered on it.
Duplicate. If brand + name match a tobacco you already own, a banner warns you — without blocking (a new vintage is legitimate). For a plain new tin, open the existing entry and add a lot via Add a lot.
Collections (free-text tags). The tobacco form has a Collections section where you assign your own tags ("travel", "gifts", "carry"…). One tag can group as many tobaccos as you like. In the inventory, a row of Collections chips filters the list by a tag; on the fiche the tags fold behind the "Collections · N" label — unfold it and tapping a tag opens the filtered list, and global search matches your tags.

6. Managing lots

Open a tobacco's page (tap its card) to see all its lots. Use Add a lot to record a new purchase — a single tobacco can hold several lots in parallel (one tin cellaring, one jar in use). Show finished displays the history of finished lots.

Each lot row shows, at a glance, its status, its remaining weight (coloured chip) and its key date: "cellared" / "jarred" / "ended". Tap the row for the full detail, then Edit (status, weight, dates) or Delete.

The lot detail also lists the sessions smoked from that lot — date and weight, most recent first. Tap a row to open the session; back returns you to the lot.

Initial vs current weight. At creation you enter the original weight; the remaining weight then evolves on its own as you log sessions. The initial weight stays as a reference (manually correctable).
Auto-reactivation. If you edit or delete a session and that gives weight back to a Finished lot, it returns to Jar on its own — except when it is marked 🚮 Disposed: that one gets its weight back but stays finished, since the tin is no longer in your hands. (An opened jar, however, never reverts to cellar automatically.)
Delete = 30-day trash. The lot isn't erased: it goes to the trash, recoverable for 30 days (see Trash). Linked sessions reattach if you restore it.
Seller's website. Beyond price and seller, a lot can carry a Seller's website field (URL). Once filled in, it shows as a clickable link on the fiche. The same field exists on pipes and accessories.
Record several lots at once. When creating a lot, the Number of lots field lets you record several identical lots in one go — handy when you buy the same tobacco in multiple tins. If the box number is numeric, it auto-increments (5, 6, 7…).
Duplicate a lot. From an existing lot (its Edit button), the Duplicate button creates a fresh copy — another unit of the same purchase: same price, seller and dates, weight reset to full, and the next box number. Ideal to complete a bulk purchase one lot at a time.

7. Browsing the inventory

Global search

The magnifying-glass icon, at the top of every list, opens a cross-section search. Type at least 2 characters: results are grouped by section (Tobaccos · Pipes · Wishlist · Accessories · Sessions · Catalog) and cover name, brand, blend, category, cut and notes. Tap a result to go there: a tobacco, a pipe or an accessory opens its page; a session opens the page of the tobacco smoked; a wish takes you back to its card in the wishlist, highlighted for a few seconds (a wish has no page of its own — the card carries the whole record). Search also queries your reference catalogue, when you have loaded one: a "Catalog" result opens the catalog pre-filtered, ready to add the tobacco to your inventory.

Status filters

Above the list, a horizontally-scrollable row of chips filters the inventory. Always visible: All (the whole inventory, including tobaccos with no active lot), Active (default — at least one cellar or jar lot), In cellar, In jar, Finished lot (tobaccos with at least one finished lot), Wishlist (toggles to your wishes — see section 9), Recent buys (lots bought in the last 90 days). Others only appear when relevant: Used up (fully smoked — all lots finished, a rebuy candidate), No lot (a tobacco whose last lot you deleted), Disposed (lots thrown away 🚮), Don't rebuy, Low stock, plus the maturity bands — cellar lots only — Young, Optimal, Near peak and Overaged, plus Smoke soon, which is those last two together — it is the chip the Home tile of the same name opens.

The funnel: the other filters

The funnel icon in the tools row — the same on all three lists (tobaccos, pipes, accessories) — folds away the filters you don't use every day, because they used to take a whole row above the first card. It stays lit as long as a hidden filter is active, so a narrowed list can never look complete. Under the inventory's funnel:

  • three dropdowns, Type, Cut and Rating;
  • your Collections (free-text tags — see section 5): one chip per tag ("travel", "gifts"…).

They all compose with each other and with the status chips, and each one stays visible as a pill even when the funnel is folded away.

Filter by aromas

The diamond icon, next to the funnel, opens the aroma wheel as a filter: pick one or more descriptors and the list keeps only the tobaccos whose own sessions carry every one of them. So it filters on your experience, not on the entry: it can only find tobaccos you have smoked and annotated (see Session Journal). The selected aromas show as pills above the list.

Sort and grouping

Sort by Name · Brand · Rating · Age · Quantity · Strength · Room Note · Taste. The list is grouped by brand (alphabetical), each group collapsed by default — tap a header to expand. The two icon buttons at the top-right switch between grouped/flat list and detailed/compact card.

Shopping list

When a tobacco drops below the low-stock threshold (see Preferences) and you haven't marked it "don't rebuy", a cart icon appears in the inventory's top bar (there only — not on the Home screen). It opens the Shopping list: tobaccos to restock (low stock) and your wishlist wishes, each with a "got it" checkbox and a button to copy the list. Tapping a row opens the tobacco's fiche or the wishlist.

Swipe to go back: swiping from the left edge of the screen closes the current detail and returns to the list. Works in all views.

Suggestions on the Home screen

The Home screen makes use of your inventory with no setup on your part:

  • "Tonight?" features one tobacco and offers up to 3 more to smoke, combining aging urgency, nearly-empty jars, time since your last session and your rating. Each suggestion shows its reasons; a tap opens the page. The main card also carries a "Pair with" pill — the pipe suggested for that tobacco: it is tappable and opens the pipe's page, whereas the rest of the card opens the tobacco's. That pipe is drawn from every active pipe rested long enough — 14 days untouched is plenty, past that no pipe is "more rested" than another — and the draw changes each time you open the app. If one of them is dedicated to the suggested tobacco's family (at least 3 sessions, most of them in that family), it is preferred and carries the "matched" tag.
  • "Watch list" gathers (up to 3 at a time) the tobaccos that deserve attention: lots overaged or at their peak, tobaccos nearly out of stock (except those marked "don't rebuy"). The low-stock threshold is set in Settings → Preferences → Watch list (25 g by default).
  • "At their peak" is the positive counterpart: the tobaccos with at least one lot in its optimal window and none at its peak or overaged — the ones it is time to enjoy, with no urgency. A tap opens the tobacco's page on those lots; the page names the slice and offers "Show all" to bring the rest back. The watch-list rows do the same when their chip names a band.
  • "Your profile" summarises your favourite families (categories ranked by average rating) and suggests your best matching tobaccos; tapping a family opens the filtered inventory. The "Families" section next to it shows your cellar's breakdown by category.

These lists disappear when there's nothing to flag.

Among the Home screen's number tiles, "Smoke soon" counts lots, not tobaccos: those of the last two maturity bands together, Near peak and Overaged — the same total as the "Cellar maturity" bar just above it. A tap opens the inventory on those lots, so it lists the tobaccos carrying them, often fewer than the lots themselves.

Lot maturity (cellar only): only cellar (sealed) lots are classified by age into four bands — Young, Optimal, Near peak, Overaged. A jar lot (opened) isn't classified — it shows "Open · duration" instead. The threshold depends on the blend: if it carries an “aging age” (which you enter or the AI suggests), that drives the calculation; otherwise a per-family default applies, calibrated from specialist sources (Latakia and aromatics mature fast and are flagged early, while Virginia and Perique keep longest). Maturity shows per cellar lot in the tobacco fiche and its detail modal, and the inventory card shows the full cellar-lot distribution with counts (e.g. “Young · 2 · Overaged · 1”). On the home page, the “Cellar maturity” tile shows an estimated autonomy at the bottom — how long your stock will last, computed from the remaining weight and your recent smoking pace (hidden if weight accounting is off).

8. Pipe collection

On the Pipes tab, tap the + at the top to create a pipe; tap a card to open its page, and the page's Edit button to work on it again. The AI assistant can retrieve the shape and the chamber dimensions from the brand and the model (see AI Assistant).

FieldDescription
Brand * / Model *Pipe identification — both are required: as long as either one is missing, the Save button stays greyed out
ShapeBilliard & cylindrical: Barrel, Belge, Bent Billiard, Billiard, Canadian, Cherrywood, Chimney, Diplomat, Duke, Liverpool, Lovat, Lumberman, Panel, Poker, Pot, Stack
Apple & round: Apple, Author, Ball, Bent Apple, Brandy, Egg, Prince, Round Apple, Tomato, Wide Apple
Dublin & conical: Acorn, Cutty, Dublin, Horn, Pear, Pickaxe, Tulip, Volcano, Woodstock, Zulu
Bulldog & Rhodesian: Bent Bulldog, Bullcap, Bulldog, Bullmoose, Rhodesian, Straight Rhodesian
Calabash: Calabash, Gourd Calabash, Reverse Calabash
Long & short stems: Churchwarden, Nose Warmer
Fancy & freehand: Blowfish, Cavalier, Cobra, Dawes, Elephant Foot, Freehand, Hawkbill, Nautilus, Oval, Sitter, Skater, Ukelele, Vest Pocket

Other: any shape not listed
BendStraight · Semi-bent · Bent
