Chamber diam. (mm)Inner diameter of the bowl chamber in millimetres — retrievable via AI
Chamber depth (mm)Depth of the bowl chamber in millimetres — retrievable via AI
Bowl materialClay · Bamboo · Briar · Cherry wood · Oak · Maple · Corn cob · Meerschaum · Metal · Morta · Walnut · Olive wood · Bone · Soapstone · Pear wood · Porcelain · Other
Stem materialAcrylic · Amber · Wood · Reed · Horn · Cumberland · Delrin · Ivorite · Lucite · Bone · Ebonite · Other
FinishSmooth · Rusticated · Sandblasted · Stained · Other — the surface treatment of the briar
Purchase dateUsed to calculate age
Production dateVintage or manufacturing date of the pipe
Price / SellerValue and provenance
Rating (★)0 to 5 stars
DescriptionGeneral notes about the pipe
Personal notesYour private remarks (maintenance, anecdotes…)
CollectionsYour own free-text tags, as on tobaccos — see section 5
StatusActive / Retired (retired ones are hidden by default)

The detail view shows all fields (even empty ones, as —). To retire a pipe or bring one back, open the edit form and flip the status. Retired pipes are hidden by default.

Several photos per pipe. Beyond the cover photo, a pipe accepts up to 6 additional photos (Add a photo button in the form). The fiche then shows a small gallery; tap a thumbnail to open it full-screen. These extra photos are pipes-only and are included in your backups.

A filter bar (Shape, Brand, Family, Rating) narrows the list; filters compose with an AND and show up as chips you clear with the ×. The Family filter is not about the pipe but about what you have smoked in it — it finds "my Latakia pipes" from your journal — and its "Unsmoked" option isolates the pipes that have no session yet. The list is grouped by brand, each group collapsed by default. The icon buttons at the top-right switch the display, and the funnel folds away the collection chips just as on the inventory.

Pipe rest: every active pipe that's been smoked at least once shows a "rest N d" badge — days since its last session. Green from 2 days of rest (ready to smoke), amber if smoked recently (let it dry). Computed automatically from your journal; no data entry. Additionally, if you pick a pipe smoked less than 24 h ago in a session or tasting, a warning reminds you it hasn't rested (advisory — never blocks saving).
On the Home screen: the pipe of the moment is your most-used pipe over the last 30 days (failing a recent session, the highest-rated active pipe). The "oldest" stat shows the age of your oldest active pipe, from its production date (or purchase date).
Families smoked, and ghosting. A pipe's page carries a "Families smoked" section: one bar per tobacco family you have smoked in it, derived from your journal — nothing to enter. It answers the question "what has this pipe really seen?", which memory judges badly.
Some families mark the briar: Latakia, Balkan, English, Aromatic and Oriental blends leave a taste the pipe will hand on to the next tobacco — that is ghosting. When a pipe is clearly dedicated to one of them — at least 3 logged sessions, of which at least 60% in the same family — the page flags it with a 👻 note.
The same warning comes back at the moment it is useful: if you pick that pipe for a session or a tasting with a tobacco from another family, it appears under the pipe picker. It never prevents saving — it is a reminder, not a rule, and plenty of smokers are quite happy with an all-round pipe. Conversely, a pipe shared half and half between two families is dedicated to neither and triggers nothing.
Maintenance log: the pipe fiche has a “Maintenance log” — each session picks a cleaning type (light, full, or “no cleaning” for a stand-alone intervention) then ticks the tasks done (pipe cleaner, bowl, reaming, salt & alcohol, stem, wax, repair, rest, other), with the date and notes. Entries are shown newest first. A “cleaning due” reminder appears (a chip on the card and fiche, plus a section at the bottom of the home page) once a pipe has been smoked a set number of sessions since its last cleaning — only the light and full cleanings reset the counter (“no cleaning” doesn't count). Every entry carries a date and a time: the time is what lets a bowl smoked on the same day as the cleaning be counted — without it, a pipe cleaned and then smoked again within the day would report nothing. An entry with no time (older ones, or if you clear the field) counts as though it happened at noon. Threshold adjustable in Settings → Preferences (5 sessions by default). You can also turn these reminders off entirely via Settings → Preferences → “Maintenance reminders”: the reminders then disappear from the home page and pipe fiches, but the log stays available. The log is included in your backups.

9. Wishlist

A memo for tobaccos to buy. On the Tobaccos tab, tap the Wishlist chip (heart icon) in the filter row to show only your wishes; pick any other filter to return to the inventory. The wishlist is part of your inventory — these are your own wishes, not catalogue entries.

Fastest way to fill your wishlist: the reference catalogue (📖 icon, see section 4) offers a ★ Add to my wishlist button on every blend — the wish is created pre-filled in one tap, no typing. You can also add a wish manually with the + button; the AI assistant and tin scan work on that form too.

Each entry can contain:

FieldDescription
Name / BrandIdentification of the desired tobacco
Type / CutDesired category and cut
Strength ★Nicotine strength — 1 star (mild) to 5 (very full)
Room Note ★Olfactory pleasantness for bystanders — 1 to 5 stars
Taste ★Flavour intensity on the palate — 1 to 5 stars
BlendExpected leaf varieties
DescriptionGeneral information
Max cellar age (yrs)Recommended maximum aging duration, e.g. 5 or 3-5
Tobacco notesYour personal impressions of this tobacco
ImageReference photo
PriorityHigh / Medium / Low — to sort your wishes by importance
CommentsPrivate remark (where to buy, observed price…)

Item actions

  • Edit — tap the wish card.
  • Acquire (box icon) — converts to a pre-filled tobacco entry (Name, Brand, Type, Cut, Blend, Description, Max age, Notes) and removes it from the wishlist. Just add your lots.
  • Delete (trash icon) — sends the entry to the trash (recoverable for 30 days, see section 17).

The AI assistant's Search button also fills the fields from the name and brand (see section 12). The wishlist is grouped by brand and accepts type / cut filters.

Duplicate detection: as you type, an amber banner (non-blocking) warns you if an identical wish already exists, or if the tobacco is already in your inventory — in which case better to open its fiche and add a lot.

10. Accessories

Track your smoking accessories: lighters, tobacco pouches, pipe tampers, pipe stands, etc. Accessible from the Acc. tab in the navigation bar (the page is titled Workshop). Tap the + at the top to create one, and a card to open its page.

FieldDescription
Name / BrandAccessory identification
TypeLighter · Tobacco pouch · Pipe tamper · Pipe stand · Other
FuelGas · Petrol · Electric · Matches · Other
Purchase date / Price / SellerPurchase history
Rating (★)0 to 5 stars
NotesPersonal remarks
ImagePhoto
CollectionsYour own free-text tags, as on tobaccos — see section 5
StatusActive / Retired — retired accessories are hidden by default

The list is grouped by type, each group collapsed by default, with a brand filter. Two chips Active / Retired filter by status: retired accessories are hidden by default — tap Retired to show them. The funnel folds away the collection chips, just as on the inventory.

Name or brand: fill in at least one of the two fields — a generic unbranded tamper is perfectly valid.

11. Session Journal

Log each smoking session to build a personal history. Accessible from the Journal tab. Two ways to add a session: the + at the top, to record after the fact a session already smoked (date and time are pre-filled with the current moment); or the flame icon next to it, which times the session live — see Tasting mode below.

FieldDescription
DateSession date (required)
Start timeOptional time (HH:MM) — orders same-day sessions; pre-filled with the current time on a new session
TobaccoSelect a tobacco from your inventory
PipeSelect a pipe from your collection
Duration (min)Session length in minutes
Weight (g)Weight of tobacco used for the session — automatically deducted from the selected jar lot on save. The default value is configurable in Settings (gear icon, top right) → Preferences
Lot usedThe lot the weight will come out of. Shown as soon as the selected tobacco has at least one usable lot, and it lists Jar and Cellar lots — each prefixed with its status. Picking a cellar lot triggers the opening confirmation described below
Rating (★)0 to 5 stars for this session
NotesImpressions, conditions, free notes
AromasAroma wheel (multi-select chips, 6 families × 5 descriptors) — the aromas perceived during the session, aggregated into the "Aroma profile" in Statistics and on the tobacco's page
LocationOptional geographic location (Add my location button) — see "Session location" below
The whole wheel. All 30 descriptors, by family — Sweet: Caramel, Honey, Vanilla, Chocolate, Molasses · Fruity: Citrus, Dried fruit, Fig, Plum, Berry · Spicy: Pepper, Clove, Nutmeg, Cinnamon, Anise · Earthy & woody: Earthy, Leather, Wood, Hay, Mushroom · Smoky & roasted: Smoky, Peat, Campfire, Tar, Incense · Floral & vegetal: Floral, Grassy, Herbal, Tea, Nutty.
Automatic deduction. The tobacco selector lists those with at least one usable lot: a Jar or Cellar lot whose weight is positive or not yet entered. The distinction matters, because a lot with no weight filled in is the default state of a freshly created tobacco: the tin is open on your desk, and the app has to let you log a bowl. Only a 0 typed by hand means "empty tin" and takes the lot out of the choice. On save, the amount is deducted from the lot; editing or deleting the session later corrects the lot automatically. If you pick a cellar lot (sealed tin), a confirmation offers to open it (transition to jar, today's date) before deducting. When a jar reaches 0, it turns to Finished — ✓ Consumed on its own; a discreet note under the Weight field warns you just before.
Editable tobacco notes: as soon as a tobacco is picked, its notes appear in an editable field under the selector — you can extend them while writing, saved to the entry when you leave the field.

Sessions are grouped by year then month (collapsed by default). A sort selector orders the list by date, tobacco, pipe, duration or rating. Five combinable selectors narrow it — pipe, tobacco, year, and, as soon as a session carries the information, country and locality; they only offer entities actually used. A Date filter (exact day) is added when you arrive from the Statistics calendar — it shows as a chip and is exclusive of the year filter.

Tasting mode (live session)

To time a session in real time, tap the Start a tasting button (flame icon) in the top bar of the Journal. If a session is already running, the Home screen shows a ▶ Resume tasting button that brings you back to the full-screen view.

  1. Setup: pick the tobacco (open jars, but also a tin still sealed in the cellar — the app then asks you to confirm opening it), the pipe (active ones only), the lot as soon as there is at least one usable, and the planned amount smoked (pre-filled with your default session weight).
  2. Light up: tap Light up to start the timer. Preparation/lighting time is not counted.
  3. During the session: a giant timer is displayed full-screen along with the chosen tobacco and pipe images. You can Pause and resume at will, adjust the rating (★), take quick notes, or change the amount smoked.
  4. Background: if you switch to another menu while the tasting is running, the timer keeps going. A gold "Tasting in progress" banner stays pinned at the top of every other view with the live elapsed time — tap ▶ Resume to return to the full-screen view.
  5. End: tap the square End button (to the right of Pause). A session is automatically created in the journal, dated from the moment you lit up and not from the moment you end — so a session straddling midnight, or forgotten for a few hours, still lands on the right day —, with the duration rounded to minutes, the tobacco, the pipe and the amount, which is deducted from the jar exactly as for a manual session.
Robust: the screen stays on during a tasting (Wake Lock API). If the app restarts or the tab closes, the session is restored on the next open — no time lost, even if an update fires meanwhile. The update countdown is in fact suspended while a tasting is running.
90-minute safety net: after 90 min, a red banner asks whether you're still smoking. With no answer within 5 min, the session closes and saves on its own. A session left paused follows exactly the same rule: 90 min of pause, then 5 min to answer. The duration saved is still the time actually smoked. Keep going defers the alert by another 90 min.
The Cancel button asks for confirmation: elapsed time is lost and nothing is created in the journal.

Session location

You can attach a location to a session, from the add form, the tasting setup, or while the timer is running ("📍" strip below the notes). Capture is never automatic: your browser asks for permission the first time.

Automatic place name. On capture, the app derives the location from the coordinates — no AI, via OpenStreetMap. It is stored as three editable fields: Place, Town/City, Country (kept separate to allow stats by town or country later) and shown merged ("Blue Note, New York, USA"). All three stay editable in the session form. In the journal, the list shows only the merged name; the map shows when you open the session detail (small OpenStreetMap map + "Open map" button). Editable coordinates: type or correct the latitude and longitude by hand — handy for a session logged after the fact, without GPS; the Add my location button is still a shortcut that fills those two fields. An Update address button then fills Place / Town / Country from those coordinates (OpenStreetMap).

Privacy: only that session's coordinates are sent to OpenStreetMap — to render the map and to derive the place name (the nominatim.openstreetmap.org service). No other data is transmitted, and if you never attach a location, no request is made.

12. AI Assistant

Available in tobacco, pipe and wishlist forms. Fill in the name or brand, then tap Search. The app automatically fills category, blend, cut or shape, description — and for tobaccos: Strength, Room Note, Taste and Max cellar age.

The AI and the catalogue answer the same question by two different routes. The catalogue is your file: instant, offline, free, but it only knows what you put in it. The AI agent queries an online provider: it knows blends your file does not carry, but it asks for a key, a connection, and it is billed by your provider. The setting below decides which of the two is consulted first.

Preferred source

Set in Settings → Data → AI Assistant → Preferred source:

  • AI agent — queries your provider first, with a silent fallback to the catalogue on error.
  • Catalogue (default) — instant, offline, no key. Pick this without an API key, or to save your quota. With no catalogue loaded there is nothing to consult, and the app tells you so.

After a fill from the catalogue, a "Cross-check with AI" button gets a second opinion and shows a diff of the divergent fields — you then pick Apply AI or Keep catalogue.

AI agent (providers)

The key does not come with the app. You first open an account with the provider of your choice, generate an API key there, then paste it here. That provider bills you for what you use — the app never does. Without a key the AI agent can do nothing; the catalogue answers with no key and no connection.

ProviderWhere to get the key
Anthropic (default)console.anthropic.com
OpenAIplatform.openai.com
Google Geminiaistudio.google.com

Configure in Settings → Data → AI Assistant → choose provider → paste key → Save.

Model choice. Below the provider, a Model dropdown lets you pick which model is used. It sits on Auto by default: the app takes the vendor's cheapest model and switches by itself if that one is retired — nothing for you to watch, and the dropdown names the model that actually runs. You can also pin a specific one, on the Claude side for instance: Haiku (fast and cheap), Sonnet or Opus (sharper but slower and pricier). Each provider keeps its own choice, remembered for next time. A Check the model button under the dropdown (and an automatic check when you change model) tells you straight away whether the model responds with your key, instead of finding out at your first search; that check costs nothing. If a pinned model has been retired, the search says so plainly and you just pick another — on Auto, the app handles it itself.
Web search — with Anthropic, the agent searches the internet before answering: it is asked to rely on specialist pipe-tobacco sources (shops, review databases) and to leave a field empty rather than invent one. OpenAI and Gemini do not search: they answer from their internal knowledge, which can give a different result for a recent or obscure blend.
Your key is stored locally. By default it is excluded from exports and backups; to include it, disable Exclude API keys from exports in the same section.

Scan the tin

In the tobacco (and wish) form, the Scan the tin button lets you photograph the tin (or pick a photo from your library): the AI reads the label to fill the brand, the name and the composition when readable, then automatically chains the usual search to complete the rest. One photo, a complete sheet. The photo is downscaled before upload and never stored. If it's unreadable, a message invites you to retry; nothing is overwritten.

The reference catalogue — getting one, loading it, browsing it, applying its data — has a section 4 of its own. For the three ways to create a tobacco, see section 5.

13. Statistics and charts

Accessible via the Stats button (chart icon) in the navigation bar. Displays SVG charts computed in real time from your data.

ChartDescription
Donut by typeActive stock (cellar + jars) by category (g or oz per your preference in the Settings), with colour legend — tap a segment or a legend row to filter the inventory by category
Top brandsHorizontal bars — active weight by brand (top 10) — tap to filter the inventory by brand
Rating distributionHistogram ★1 to ★5 across all your rated tobaccos — tap a bar to filter the inventory to that exact rating
Pipes by shapeHorizontal bars — number of active pipes by shape — tap to filter the pipe list
Bowl / stem materialBreakdown of pipes by bowl and stem material (side by side) — tap a bar to filter the pipe list by that material
Locations (towns / countries)Number of sessions per town and per country (side by side), from the geolocated session place — only the town and country are counted (the exact spot name is ignored). The country shows with its flag. Tapping a bar opens the journal filtered on that town or country. Only shows when at least one session carries a town or country
Duration by monthTotal session time (in hours) over the last 12 months — only shown when sessions with a duration exist — tap to open the journal filtered on the matching year
SpendingAll-time total + current-year total, per-kind breakdown (tobacco / pipes / accessories), a monthly and a yearly histogram per kind. Computed from the prices and purchase dates you already entered
Tobacco smoked by monthTotal tobacco weight consumed (g or oz) over the last 12 months, by month — tap to open the journal filtered on the matching year
Duration by yearTotal session time (in hours) per calendar year — tap to open the journal filtered on that year
Tobacco smoked by yearTotal tobacco weight consumed (g or oz) per calendar year — tap to open the journal filtered on that year
Top tobaccos smokedTop 10 by total weight smoked (g or oz), number of sessions in parentheses — tap to open the tobacco detail directly and expand its brand group in the inventory
Top pipes usedTop 10 by number of sessions, total time in parentheses — tap to open the pipe detail directly and expand its brand group in the list
Average session lengthMean computed over every session with a recorded duration — quick marker for your usual smoking pace
Rating by tobacco ageAverage session rating by the tobacco's age when it was smoked, bucketed into cellaring bands (< 1 yr, 1-2, 2-4, 4-7, 7-10, 10 yrs +) — reveals the window where your tobaccos are at their best. A per-family filter refines the curve
Aroma profileBars of the aromas most perceived across your sessions, aggregated from the journal's aroma wheel, with a per-family filter
Taste profileBars averaged over the tobaccos you rated ≥ 4 stars (Strength · Room Note · Taste) — what you actually enjoy
Cost per sessionReal cost of one bowl per tobacco: grams smoked × (lot price ÷ lot weight), averaged over the tobacco's sessions, plus a global average. Only counts fully measurable sessions (grams > 0, a resolved lot with a price and weight). Tap a row to open the tobacco's fiche
Smoking calendarHeatmap over the last 12 months: each square is a day, the colour goes from sage (low) to amber and then oxblood (high) based on the number of sessions. Tap a cell to display the detail under the calendar; for a day with at least one session, the info line becomes a tappable button "DD.MM.YYYY · N sessions →" that opens the journal filtered on that exact date
Charts are hidden until matching data exists; they populate as your collection grows.
Clickable Statistics: almost every block is a one-tap drill-down into its source list. Category / brand / shape / material → filtered list. Month or year → pre-filtered journal. Calendar day → journal filtered on that day. Town or country → journal filtered on that location. Top tobacco or top pipe → its detail view.

14. Backup, export and settings

Export and backup are in Settings → Data; the other settings have tabs of their own (see Settings in 4 tabs, below). Two backup methods, to combine freely: local export (no account) and cloud backup (Dropbox or Google Drive).

Your settings travel with the backup

Every export and every cloud backup also carries your preferences: language, theme, light/dark mode, text size, date format, currency, units, accounting, alert thresholds, default session weight, list grouping, wishlist sort, visible sections, AI provider and model, cloud destination. Restoring onto a new phone therefore gives you your cellar and your settings, with nothing to reconfigure.

They are applied only if you choose “Replace” when restoring. A merge never touches your settings: combining two cellars is no reason to adopt the other device's language and theme.
No secret is exported. Your Dropbox and Google Drive sign-in tokens never leave the device, and your API key stays excluded by default (a checkbox in Settings → Data → AI if you want to include it). The device name and id stay local too, so two devices never fight over the same auto-save file.

JSON / ZIP export (no account)

  • 📋 Export JSON — all your data and photos in a single file, fully re-importable via 📂 Import file. This is the complete backup method if you don't use the cloud.
  • 📦 Export ZIP — same content, plus a CSV and photos as separate files (handy for archiving).
  • 📄 Export CSV — tobaccos, pipes and wishlist as a spreadsheet (Excel), for external use.
  • Collection report — a printable HTML document of your whole valued collection: every tobacco, pipe and accessory with its purchase value, the weight split into cellar and jar for tobaccos, per-section subtotals and a grand total. Ideal for insurance or archiving. Values are an acquisition-cost baseline (not a market appraisal).
  • 📂 Import file — restore from a JSON file (Replace / Merge / Select, see below).
  • Import tobaccos (.csv) — adds tobaccos (and their lots) from a spreadsheet. Columns are matched by header name (accents and case ignored, FR/EN aliases): only Brand and Name are required. Rows sharing the same brand + name collapse into one multi-lot tobacco. The import always runs as a Merge (never a replace: your pipes and sessions are never wiped, and duplicates are de-duplicated on brand + name). Use Download CSV template to start from a ready-to-fill file. The Category and Cut columns must use the app's own taxonomy (lists in section 5) — common trade labels are recognised (« Navy Cut », « Cigar »), an unknown value is set to « Autre », and the detail appears under the button with the line number.
Two different CSV files are loaded from this screen. Do not confuse them.
  • Import tobaccos (.csv), above — these are your tins. Columns Brand, Name, weight, price, dates. The file is added to your cellar by merging and creates lots. Its template is the Download CSV template button, in this same section.
  • Load a catalogue (.csv), in the Reference catalogue section above — these are descriptive entries. Columns brand_key, blend_name, category, cut… The file replaces the previous catalogue and adds nothing to your cellar. Its template is the Download the template button, in that other section. See section 4.
The two formats have neither the same columns nor the same effect: loading one in place of the other destroys nothing, but the app will tell you it could not read anything.
Explicit CSV-import errors. If the file holds no readable tobacco, a message reminds you of the expected columns. If you pick a JSON backup file by mistake, the app points you to Import file (.json). A full multi-section export CSV is accepted: only its tobacco part is imported, with a note.
Replace, Merge or Select. After you pick the file, a picker offers three modes: Replace (wipes your local data and installs the imported file), Merge (keeps your cellar and pours into it what the file brings) or Select items (you pick exactly which tobaccos / pipes / wishes / accessories / sessions to import). The same picker appears on a cloud restore, including when you tap Restore on the Home screen's "newer backup" banner: nothing is overwritten before you have chosen the mode.
Exactly what "Merge" does, because "it only adds things" would be reassuring and false. An entry you don't have is added. An entry you already hold is matched with its twin — first by its internal identifier, and only failing that by brand + name — then, if the imported copy is newer than yours, its descriptive fields (category, cut, blend, strength, description, rating…) replace yours. That is what carries a correction made on one device over to the other. Your personal notes and your collections escape that rule: they are only written where you had nothing. Missing lots are added to the matching tobacco, and sessions are de-duplicated. A recap lists everything that happened.
The Replace mode overwrites all your local data. Export a backup first if in doubt, or use Merge.
An API key contained in the backup is only installed in "Replace" mode. A merge never installs it — merging is how you accept someone else's file, and their key would then become yours: your AI searches billed to their account, and your own key overwritten. Same rule as for the preferences, which a restore adopts and a merge does not.
Delete all data — the last button of the section, in red. It returns the device to a clean slate, after a confirmation that names exactly what goes: the cellar (tobaccos, pipes, accessories, wishes, sessions), the photos stored locally, the cloud connection (Dropbox or Google Drive), the AI assistant's API keys and every preference (language, theme, units, thresholds…). This is the button to use before lending or selling the device, or before posting a screenshot of it. The operation cannot be undone. Your cloud backups and the files you have already exported are not deleted: that is where you can start again from.

☁️ Cloud backup (Dropbox recommended, or Google Drive)

Cloud backup writes to a private, dedicated folder in your account (hidden among your other files), via a direct OAuth connection. Advantages: auto-save, up to 3 manual backups + 1 auto per device, photos included, accessible from any device. The destination is chosen in Settings → Data → Cloud backup.

Dropbox is recommended (and the default). Its session never expires: the token renews automatically and silently on every platform, iPhone included — no "Reconnect" banner, no interruption. Google Drive stays available and feature-equivalent, but on iPhone its token expires (~1 h) and is renewed by tapping a save action. The buttons always carry the active destination's name (Save to Dropbox / Save to Drive).
More than a backup: sync several devices. Connect the same cloud account (Dropbox or Drive) on your phone and tablet. Each device auto-saves, and on launch, if another device saved a newer version, a banner offers to restore it — one tap and this device catches up. Your cellar follows you from device to device: it's not just a safety copy, it's a shared state. (Tip: on the second device, do a first Restore to pull in the existing cellar.)
  • Save — creates a new manual backup (💾). The last 3 are kept; older ones are pruned.
  • Restore — lists the available backups, sorted most-recent-first, with their type (💾 manual / 🔄 auto), date and the tobacco / pipe / wish / accessory / session counts (read straight from the filename). Tap a backup then Confirm.
  • View my backups — read-only panel listing the cloud files. For each one: its kind (🔄 auto, 💾 manual, 📖 catalogue), its size, its date, and the reason it would be proposed or ignored at launch (this device, older, already dismissed…). The panel also gives the total occupied, the date of your last local edit, and a roll-up by device: for each one, its last save and file count — handy to reason about which way a sync should go between two devices. A trash 🗑 on each row deletes a single backup (inline confirmation).
  • Switch account / Disconnect — on Drive, re-authenticate with another account; on Dropbox, revoke access. Your local data is unaffected.
  • Auto cloud backup (toggle) — whenever data changes, a silent auto-save (🔄) runs in the background after a few seconds, and also when you leave the app. Each device keeps its own auto file and overwrites its own each time; other devices' files are never touched. The last save timestamp — specific to the active destination — shows below the toggle.
How many files on the cloud? 3 manual backups in rotation + 1 auto file per device. One device ⇒ 4 files max; phone + tablet ⇒ up to 5 (3 manual + 2 auto). If you have sent your catalogue to the cloud, one more file joins them — just one, marked 📖. It is not part of the backup rotation: it only leaves when you ask, a new version replaces the previous one, and restoring your cellar does not touch it.
Auto-save diagnostic: since auto-save is silent, a small line appears below the toggle only when an auto-save has failed and tells you why (e.g. ✗ token unavailable — tap Save to renew on iPhone + Drive). When everything is fine, no line is shown. If the token is unavailable, a manual save (or a tap on Reconnect) gets it going again. And if several backups fail in a row, an amber notice tells you so: your cellar stays on the device, only the cloud copy is missing.
Replace mode erases all your local data before installing the file — it is the picker's default mode, and it cannot be undone. Merge keeps your cellar (see above for exactly what it does). If in doubt, export to JSON first.
An amber warning is shown if the selected backup contains fewer entries than your local data — a sign it may have been taken before you added them.
Expired Drive session: on Google Drive, the session lasts ~1 h. If a change is pending and the token has expired, an amber banner appears: tap Reconnect (your account is pre-selected, one tap is enough). On iPhone, the app uses your save taps to renew the token silently — the banner only surfaces on a real failure. With Dropbox, this case doesn't exist.

🔒 Encrypt cloud backups (optional)

In Settings → Data → Cloud backup, the Encrypt cloud backups toggle protects your backups with a passphrase (8 characters min.). Every backup is encrypted locally (AES-GCM-256 + PBKDF2) before upload, whichever destination is active (Dropbox or Google Drive): a third party reading your folder would see only opaque data.

The passphrase is never stored and cannot be recovered. Save it in a password manager before enabling — without it, your encrypted backups will be unreadable.
The passphrase lives in memory for the session. After a reload, Settings shows "🔒 Encryption active · passphrase needed" with a button to re-enter it. The restore flow auto-detects encrypted backups and prompts when needed; old plaintext backups remain restorable without one.
Viewer (lightbox): tap any image to view it full-screen. A long press opens the native menu — "Save to Photos" on iOS, "Download / Copy / Share" on Android. Tap the dark area or ✕ to close.

Photo storage

Imported photos are saved to the device's local cache (not the main storage), which keeps it from filling up. They're included in all exports and restored automatically on import. Images previously added by URL link (a removed feature) are cleared — the app keeps only local photos.

The browser may clear this cache under memory pressure — more common on iOS. If that happens, photos may disappear. Back up regularly (cloud or export) — the backup includes all your photos.

Settings in 4 tabs

  • Preferences (default tab on open) — language, text size, date format, units, currency, default session weight, list grouping, accounting, maintenance reminders, sections.
  • Data — cloud backup, export & import, AI assistant.
  • App — install the app, check for updates, diagnostic.
  • Help — links to this guide, contact, what's new, privacy, licences.

The active tab is remembered. Alert banners open Settings directly on the correct tab.

Preferences

  • Language — Français / English / Español / Deutsch / Italiano / Português. On first launch the app adopts your browser's language (falling back to English); you can change it here at any time. Each language is downloaded the first time you pick it, then kept offline: if you are offline and that language was never downloaded, the app tells you and keeps your current one.
  • Text size — Small / Medium (default) / Large; rescales the whole interface at once.
  • Theme — Brass (default), Steel blue or English (green); changes the accent colour and overall mood of the whole app.
  • Mode — Dark (default) or Light (parchment background); independent of the chosen colour theme.
  • Date format — FR (15.03.2024) or EN (Mar 15, 2024). Affects display and the CSV export.
  • Weight unit (g / oz) — applied everywhere (lots, pipe weight, journal). The default session weight is converted automatically on change.
  • Length unit (mm / in) — for pipe length.
  • Currency — € (default), $, £, CHF, JPY. Replaces the symbol in every price display.
  • Default session weight — weight suggested when adding a session (default: 3).
  • Lists grouped by default — initial display of lists: Grouped (default) or Flat. You can always toggle per-list.
  • Accounting — dedicated subsection. On (default): the app deducts the weight from the lot, marks it Finished at 0, and prompts to open a cellar lot before deducting. Off: the Weight field disappears from the session form and tasting, no deduction or auto-finish, and these sessions don't enter the smoked-weight charts. Opening a cellar lot (cellar→jar) stays active — it's a physical event.
  • Low-stock threshold — under Watch list: below this remaining weight (25 g by default), a tobacco is flagged in the Home "Watch list" section (see section 7), unless it's marked "don't rebuy". Expressed in your weight unit.
Units and currency are labels only — a value already entered is never converted back. See Where to start, where the warning belongs: before your very first entry.

Section visibility

In Settings → Preferences → Sections, toggle on/off: Accessories · Journal · Statistics. A disabled section disappears from the navigation bar and the dashboard. This setting travels in your backups like the other preferences: a restore in Replace mode will install the one from the file.

Diagnostic (data integrity)

A Diagnostic section continuously monitors the internal consistency of your data. As long as all is well it stays green. On an anomaly (a session pointing to a deleted lot, duplicate ids…) it turns oxblood and lists the latest occurrences with their technical detail. The Copy / Download buttons export a JSON (handy for support); Clear resets the counter without touching data. It's informational only: on a visible inconsistency, restore your latest backup.

Multi-device guard

If you use the app on more than one device, it silently checks at launch whether the cloud holds a backup newer than anything this device has saved. When it does, a banner on the Home screen offers a Restore button that downloads the backup and presents you with the Replace / Merge / Select picker — the banner shows up precisely when this device has work it has not yet sent, so that is where the choice matters most. You can dismiss the banner with the × — it only returns if an even newer backup appears. This launch check never opens a sign-in window: it only uses an already-valid token, otherwise it gives up silently. A "Check cloud backups" button in Settings → Data forces an immediate re-check — that one may ask you to sign in again if your Drive session has expired.

Use the same destination on all your devices. The guard only works within a single destination: each device only compares against the files of ITS active destination. If one device backs up to Dropbox and another to Google Drive, those are two independent histories — neither will see the other's data. To sync across devices, pick the same destination on each.

Name this device

In Settings → Data, you can give this device a friendly name (e.g. "iPhone", "MacBook"). It shows in the View my backups panel instead of the technical id, and it also appears in the cloud backup filenames so you can spot them at a glance. It is not part of your data, though: it is not stored inside the backup content, only in the file name.

Stable identifier. Every tobacco, pipe, accessory, wish and session carries a hidden unique identifier, kept in your backups. Cross-device merging relies on it rather than on brand + name: two tobaccos with the same name (e.g. two vintages) are no longer confused or merged during a sync, and re-importing a backup no longer creates duplicates.

Merging duplicate entries

When two entries share the same brand and the same name — the typical case for a cellar synced across two devices before stable identifiers arrived — a Duplicates (N) button appears in Settings → Data. It only shows up when there is actually something to resolve: the count is the reason to look.

For each group the panel shows what you need in order to decide: number of lots, active weight, number of sessions, inventory numbers and last edit date. When two entries share an inventory number that appears nowhere else in the cellar, it says so («probably the same tin»). You designate the one to keep: its lots and sessions take over the other's, which goes to the trash — recoverable for 30 days.

Inventory numbers are never rewritten — they are written on the physical tin. Two moved lots may therefore end up sharing a number: that is precisely the sign that they are the same tin.

15. Updates

Settings → App tab → Check for updates button (below the app name and version). If a new version is available, an Update card appears: confirm it, and the app re-downloads itself.

The update only touches the app cache: your cellar, photos and notes are untouched.

If the update fails

If the app gets stuck on the loading screen after an update, wait for a fix to be released, then tap the Nothing happening? Repair the app → link that appears automatically after a few seconds. This resets the cache and reloads the app from the network.

This repair does not delete your data. If the server cannot be reached, it declines to run and deletes nothing: reconnect, then try again.

16. Troubleshooting

App alert banners

The app shows several banners depending on context:

  • Red banner (top) — save error. Tap it to open Settings and perform an emergency JSON export. Possible causes: storage quota exhausted, browser blocking storage, private browsing.
  • Amber banner (bottom) — Drive session expired (see section 14). Two actions: Reconnect, ×.
  • Green banner (top) — backup reminder: over 30 days without any export or cloud save. Its whole surface opens Settings → Data.
  • Amber “storage almost full” banner — the device is nearing its quota. Free some space or remove photos; dismissable for 7 days.
  • Red “photo” banner — an image could not be added (over 20 MB, or an unreadable file).
  • Gold banner (top) — a tasting is running and you're on another view. Tap ▶ Resume to return to the full-screen view.
  • Red tasting banner — active session has been running for over 90 minutes (see section 11, 90-minute safety net).
  • Undo toast (8s) — shown at the bottom after every delete (tobacco, pipe, accessory, wish, session, lot). The Undo button reverses the action instantly during the 8-second window. Instant safety net, complementing the 30-day trash (see section 17).

Save error — details

Likely causes:

  • Quota exhausted — delete some photos or free space on the device.
  • iOS Safari — storage blocked: Settings → Safari → Advanced → Website Data.
  • Android Chrome — storage blocked: Chrome → Settings → Privacy and security → Site settings → Storage.
  • Private browsing — data is lost when the tab is closed; the app is not usable in private browsing.

Browser requirements

For the app to work correctly, you need:

  • JavaScript enabled (on by default in all browsers)
  • Local storage allowed — all your data is kept via localStorage. On iOS Safari: Settings → Safari → Advanced → Block All Cookies must be off. On Android Chrome: make sure cookies/storage are not blocked for this site (Chrome → Settings → Site settings). In private browsing, data is erased when you close the tab.
  • PWA installation — on iOS, use Safari only (not Chrome or Firefox): Share icon → Add to Home Screen. On Android, use Chrome: menu ⋮ → Add to Home Screen.

App stuck on the loading screen

If the loading screen doesn't go away after a few seconds, a link Nothing happening? Repair the app → appears automatically. Tap it: this clears the service worker cache and reloads the app from the network.

This repair does not delete your data — only the app cache is reset. If the server cannot be reached, it declines to run and deletes nothing: reconnect, then try again.

17. Trash (30 days)

Every delete in the app is reversible. When you tap the trash icon on a tobacco, pipe, accessory, wish, session or lot, the item isn't erased: it goes to the trash, where it stays for 30 days before automatic permanent removal.

How to open it

As soon as at least one item is in the trash, an amber trash icon appears in the top bar of every main page (Cellar, Tobacco, Pipes, Access., Journal, Stats) and on a tobacco, pipe or accessory page. Tap it to open the Trash modal. When the trash is empty, the icon disappears — no dead button.

Restore

  • Restore an item — restore icon (circular arrow) on its row. The item returns to the inventory with all its data (lots, photos, notes…).
  • Restore all — button at the bottom of the modal. Restores every item in one go.
  • Multi-select — Select button at the top of the modal switches to checkbox mode. Tick the items to restore, then tap Restore selection (N).

Permanent delete

  • × on a row — removes that item without waiting the 30 days. No confirmation: if you're unsure, use Restore instead.
  • Empty trash — button at the bottom. Confirms before wiping everything (the only confirmation kept, because the action bypasses the 30-day safety net).

Special cases

  • Sessions when a tobacco/pipe is deleted: historical sessions are kept even when their tobacco or pipe is deleted. The journal shows the photo and name as they were at the moment of deletion (frozen snapshot). Recreating a tobacco or pipe with the same name does NOT auto-re-attach old sessions (internal ids stay unique).
  • Deleted lots: sessions pointing to them keep their link as long as the trash isn't emptied. Restoring the lot reattaches the sessions cleanly.
  • Undo immediately: right after each delete, a toast at the bottom shows an Undo button for 8 seconds (instant safety net, alongside the long-term net that is the trash).
JSON and Drive backups include trash items — you keep this 30-day safety net even after a reinstall or restore. The CSV export, however, is a snapshot of the live inventory: it does not include the trash.

18. Every setting, and what it changes

An end-to-end recap, to answer "where do I set this?" without re-reading the guide. Each setting is already described in its own section, where you will find the reasoning; here you get only the place, the effect and the default. You reach them all through the gear icon at the top of the Home screen.

Preferences tab

This is the tab that opens first. Nothing here touches your data: these are display and behaviour settings, particular to this device — but they travel in your backups, and a restore in Replace mode will install the ones from the file.

  • Language — The whole interface. Each language is downloaded the first time you pick it, then stays available offline. Default: your browser's, else English.
  • Text size — Rescales the entire interface at once, not just the paragraphs. Default: Medium.
  • Theme — The accent colour and the mood: Brass, Steel blue or English. Default: Brass.
  • Mode — Dark or Light (parchment background). Independent of the theme. Default: Dark.
  • Date format — How dates are displayed and the ones in the CSV export. Default: FR (15.03.2024).
  • Weight unit — The suffix everywhere: lots, pipe weights, journal. Label only — a value already entered is not converted. Default: g.
  • Length unit — Pipe length. Label only, as above. Default: mm.
  • Currency — The symbol on every price. Label only: no exchange rate. Default: €.
  • Default session weight — The weight offered when you add a session, when the chosen pipe has no chamber dimensions. Otherwise the app estimates it from the chamber and the cut. Default: 3.
  • Lists grouped — The initial display of lists, grouped by brand or flat. You can still toggle one list at a time. Default: Grouped.
  • Accounting (lot deductions) — Whether the app tracks quantities. Off, the Weight field disappears from sessions, and there is no deduction and no automatic switch to Finished — but opening a cellar lot remains, it is a physical event. Default: On.
  • Maintenance reminders — The "cleaning due" chip on pipes and the Home section. The log stays usable even when they are off. Default: On.
  • Maintenance reminder (sessions) — The number of sessions without a cleaning before the reminder. Only the light and full cleanings reset the counter. Default: 5 sessions.
  • Low-stock threshold — Below this remaining weight, a tobacco moves into "Watch list" and into the shopping list — unless it is marked "don't rebuy". In your weight unit. Default: 25 g.
  • Sections — Hides Accessories, Journal or Statistics: the section leaves the bottom bar and the dashboard. The data stays. Default: all visible.

Data tab

Everything that brings data in or sends it out. It is the only tab whose actions cannot be undone.

  • Destination — Dropbox or Google Drive. The multi-device guard only ever compares within one destination: pick the same one on all your devices. Default: Dropbox.
  • Cloud auto-save — A silent backup leaves a few seconds after every change, and when you leave the app. Each device keeps its own file. Default: Off.
  • Encrypt cloud backups — Encrypts every cloud backup with a passphrase, before it is sent. The passphrase is never stored and cannot be recovered. Default: Off.
  • This device's name — Makes this device identifiable in the backups panel and in the name of the cloud files. Not in their contents. Default: empty.
  • Reference catalogue — Loads, exports or removes your reference file, and backs it up to the cloud separately. Never adds anything to your cellar — see section 4. Default: none.
  • AI Assistant — Provider, model, API key, and the Preferred source: your catalogue first, or the AI first. "Auto" lets the app take the cheapest model and change it if that one is retired. Default: Catalogue first; no key.
  • Exclude API keys from exports and backups — Keeps your key on this device. Leave it on if you share a file. Default: On.
  • Duplicates (N) — Only appears when there are entries to merge — the count is the reason to go and look. Default: hidden.
  • Erase all data — Empties the cellar, the photos, the API keys and the cloud connection of this device. Your backups already in the cloud are untouched. Cannot be undone. Default: —.

App tab

  • Install — How to add the app to your home screen, spelled out for your browser. Default: —.
  • Check for updates — Queries the server, then reloads the app and clears its cache. Your data is untouched. Default: —.
  • Diagnostic — Watches internal consistency. Green as long as all is well; on an anomaly it lists the latest occurrences and offers to copy them. Default: —.

Help tab

Links, no settings: this guide, what's new in the version, the privacy policy, the licences of third-party components, and a way to write to us.

What is not in Settings. A few settings live where they apply, because they concern one screen only: each list's sort and filters, the folding of groups, the wishlist order, and the collapsing of this guide's sections. They are remembered from one visit to the next, but they do not travel in your backups.
